STARHAVEN — NOTABLE PLACES IMAGE PROMPTS (for Grok image generation)
========================================================================

Still to generate: 339   (finished so far: 601)

HOW TO USE
- Size: 1536 x 1024 pixels, horizontal 3:2 (landscape). In Grok pick the landscape / 3:2 aspect.
- Save each image as PNG into  starhaven_places_image/  with the exact file name shown after "Save as".
- Places whose image already exists are left out of this file automatically.

########################################################################

=== Varathos ===

Size: 1536 x 1024 px (landscape 3:2)
Group: Atlas — Varathos   Kind: Island nation
PROMPT:
Image size: 1536 x 1024 pixels, landscape orientation, 3:2 aspect ratio.
Style: photorealistic dark-fantasy environment art, like a cinematic film still from a high-budget fantasy epic. Desaturated, moody colour grade with warm practical light wherever the place is lived-in (lanterns, hearths, lit windows). Extremely detailed textures on stone, timber, iron, glass, cloth and foliage; atmospheric depth with haze between foreground, midground and background; natural lens, no fisheye, no tilt-shift.
Subject: Varathos — the island of the giants, off the far west coast.
What it is: The giants' island, hidden behind the Varathos Veil for four thousand years until Kira's Door opened.
Wider setting — Varathos: The island of the giants, off the far west coast, hidden behind the Varathos Veil for four thousand years until the Alliance of Varathos opened Kira's Door.
Composition: an establishing view of a rugged grey island rising from a cold sea, with giant-scale stone halls, terraces and steps on its headlands and a great harbour below.
Camera angle: from the sea, from the deck of a small Starhaven trading ship arriving. (Similar places use different angles — keep to this one.)
Details to include: giant-scale granite halls and steps on the cliffs; a harbour wharf where giants unload a grain ship as easily as crates; a colossal ancient stone statue of a giant on the highest headland; a faint shimmer in the air at the harbour mouth where the Veil was opened; human sailors on the ship's deck staring up.
Environment: Varathos, the giants' island in the far west — grey granite cliffs and headlands over a cold green sea, wind-bent pines, heather and moss, sea mist, a grey-blue northern sky.
Architecture: the giants' own stone architecture — enormous plain blocks of grey granite, steps, doorways and halls built at three times human scale, ancient and weathered, with smaller stairs and doors cut into them later for human visitors; simple, massive, no ornament.
Lighting and time of day: grey morning, sun breaking through sea mist.
Mood: awe, scale, welcome.
Magic: magic is real and everyday in this world — show it subtly where it fits: rune-lit doors and seals, softly glowing mana lamps, enchanted objects, a mage at work with faint light at their hands. Never portals or rift gates.
People: a natural, varied, sparse-to-moderate crowd seen small — mixed clothes and colours, different ages and a real, clearly visible mix of races — at least half of them obviously not human: pointed-eared elves, short stocky bearded dwarves, green- and grey-skinned tusked orcs, big hulking grey-hided trolls, horned and furred beastmen, little halflings — side by side with humans, each race easy to tell apart at a glance — this is a fantasy adventurers' world, so a few adventurers in worn armour with swords, axes, bows and staves mingle with townsfolk; walking, talking, carrying things and browsing in every direction, in loose groups and gaps. Never in rows, never uniform cloaks, never all facing the camera. Clothing true to the medieval era — wool, linen, leather and fur; tunics, hoods, cloaks, aprons and boots — never modern dress.
Who lives here (Compendium → Peoples → Who lives where, “Varathos”): Giants, about twelve feet tall, in rough wool, leather and stone jewellery; also Starhaven envoys, traders and sailors of every race, tiny beside them. Show several giants and a clear mix of visiting races.
Crowd note: avoid rows of identical hooded robes or cloaks standing still and facing the same way. People wear varied everyday clothes in real colours, are of clearly different races, and are busy — walking, talking, carrying, working, laughing — in loose groups facing every direction.
Avoid: a cast of only humans; people posing together like a group portrait; portals, rift gates or glowing gateways; any text, letters, numbers or readable signage; logos, watermarks, borders, frames, UI; close-up faces or posed characters (people are small background figures for scale only); crowds standing in rows or grids, identical or uniformly cloaked figures, figures all facing the camera, army-like formations (unless the place is a military parade or drill); modern objects (cars, power lines, plastic, neon, guns, steam locomotives); churches, crosses, altars, shrines or religious symbols; architecture that reads as fully Indian, Chinese, Japanese or Persian; cartoon or painterly look; oversaturated colour.

PROMPT:

Subject: The Skyward Isles — floating archipelago, in the Skyward Isles (far south-west, beyond the storm wall).
What it is: Thirty great rocks floating above the sea inside a ring of never-ending storm, home of the Avarakai Reef Clans.
Composition: A vast establishing view across the floating islands at different heights, the ring of black storm wall on the horizon all round, the calm blue sea far below.
Camera angle: from the deck rail of a small skyship flying between the islands. (The rail and a patched sail in the foreground.) (Similar places use different angles — keep to this one.)
Details to include: thirty floating islands of different sizes, the biggest ten miles long; white waterfalls falling off their edges and turning to mist; the pink and violet sky-reef hanging under each one; dozens of small skyships with patched sails and glowing blue-white keel-stones crossing between them; a huge grey sky-whale grazing on the reef of a distant island; tiny white reed-roofed towns on top. Little moments of life: children waving from an island edge; a skyship crew hauling a net of fish up out of the sea on a long rope.
Environment: the Skyward Isles — great rough rocks floating a thousand feet above a calm, very blue sea inside a ring of black storm cloud; green and flat on top with white waterfalls pouring off their edges into mist; their pointed undersides hung with a mile of pink, white and violet coral (the sky-reef) growing down into the air; small skyships with patched sails and softly glowing blue-white keel-stones crossing between them.
Architecture: Avarakai island style, unlike Starhaven: low white houses with thick woven-reed roofs, terraced gardens, timber skyship moorings built out over the island's edge, carved stone and rope everywhere; nothing grand, no towers or spires.
Lighting and time of day: golden late afternoon, still air, the storm wall dark and flickering with lightning far off.
Mood: wondrous, vast, peaceful, a little dangerous.
Magic: magic is real and everyday in this world — show it subtly where it fits: rune-lit doors and seals, softly glowing mana lamps, enchanted objects, a mage at work with faint light at their hands. Never portals or rift gates.

Never in rows, never uniform cloaks, never all facing the camera. Clothing true to the medieval era — wool, linen, leather and fur; tunics, hoods, cloaks, aprons and boots — never modern dress.
Who lives here (Compendium → Peoples → Who lives where, “The Skyward Isles”): only beastkin of the Avarakai Reef Clans — every lineage, jaguar, ocelot, sea-otter, frigatebird, osprey, gannet, some furred and some feathered; no humans. What it is: Where every ingot is weighed, tested and sealed before it sails.
Wider setting — The Sable Gold Concession: The gold mines of the Sable Isles, worked by Starhaven under the mining rights Sorvane Nullis granted as part of the Sable Conclave alliance. The gold ships to Saltmere every month and goes on by rail to the Fortified Mint at Veynehold.
Composition: An interior view of a stone assay hall where gold ingots are weighed, tested and sealed.
Camera angle: from a raised gallery, stair or loft, looking down into the room. (Similar places use different angles — keep to this one.)

Little moments of life: an assayer holding an ingot up to the light.
Environment: inside the Assay House on the Sable Isles — dark stone, iron bars on the windows, sea-grey light.
Architecture: mostly the natural landscape; any buildings that appear are few and small, rural Starhaven, true to a real medieval countryside — modest timber-framed and stone cottages, barns and workshops, thatched or slate roofs, a stone well, a watermill or windmill where fitting, dry-stone walls, wooden fences and muddy cart tracks; buildings of one or two storeys, made from the local stone and timber the environment above describes. Environment: Tinkerton, a small toymakers’ village on the green hills just outside Vaelmark — stone-and-timber cottages with slate roofs and rune-lit doors, walled lanes and meadows; on the horizon the dark gothic spires of Vaelmark with faint blue light, and a patrol pair of ridden drakes (wyverns: two legs, wings for forelimbs) crossing the sky far off.
Architecture: a prosperous small village, not the city — cottages and workshops of one or two storeys, local stone and dark timber, a few rune-carved details; nothing grand.

Never in rows, never uniform cloaks, never all facing the camera. Clothing true to the medieval era — wool, linen, leather and fur; tunics, hoods, cloaks, aprons and boots — never modern dress.

More about it: Navid Saffrani planted the first saffron corms from a pouch he carried with the Karath gift convoy. The terraces now supply a third of the saffron sold on the Spice Steps.
Composition: a steep warm hillside cut into dozens of narrow stone-walled terraces, each a different colour — purple crocus, green ginger, red chilli, gold turmeric — with a small village of honey-coloured stone houses at the top.
Camera angle: from the valley floor below, looking up the whole stepped hillside. (Similar places use different angles — keep to this one.)
Details to include: dry-stone terrace walls; workers on the terraces with baskets; little stone stairs climbing between levels; a drying house with open shutters; a mule path zig-zagging up; the town of Sunvale glimpsed in the valley; red chillies drying on roofs.
Environment: the warm south — rolling golden fields and vineyards, honey-coloured stone and terracotta roofs, cypress and olive trees, a soft warm haze.
Architecture: rural Starhaven, true to a real medieval countryside — modest timber-framed and stone cottages, barns and workshops, thatched or slate roofs, a stone well, a watermill or windmill where fitting, dry-stone walls, wooden fences and muddy cart tracks; buildings of one or two storeys, made from the local stone and timber the environment above describes. Weathered, lived-in and practical, never grand: no towers, spires, domes or palace-like halls. Small touches from the kingdom's peoples are fine (dwarven stonework, elven carved wood) but it stays one consistent dark-fantasy kingdom.
Lighting and time of day: warm late-afternoon sun, long shadows across the terraces.
Mood: warm, colourful, quietly proud.
Magic: magic is real and everyday in this world — show it subtly where it fits: rune-lit doors and seals, softly glowing mana lamps, enchanted objects, a mage at work with faint light at their hands. Never portals or rift gates.
People: spice growers and pickers of many races, many of them Karath-born in bright head-scarves and light cotton, with local humans, halflings, dwarves and half-elves; sleeves rolled, baskets on hips. Never in rows, never posed, never all facing the camera.
Who lives here (Compendium → Peoples → Who lives where, “Farm villages”): mostly Humans and halflings; also Dwarves, elves, orcs, beastkin farmhands. The breadbasket (Ironmane, Rootholm, Amberfield and the rest): farmers, carters, market women in sturdy country clothes. Still a mixed kingdom — several races clearly visible.
Crowd note: avoid rows of identical hooded robes or cloaks standing still and facing the same way. People wear varied everyday clothes in real colours, are of clearly different races, and are busy — walking, talking, carrying, working, laughing — in loose groups facing every direction.
Avoid: a cast of only humans; people posing together like a group portrait; portals, rift gates or glowing gateways; any text, letters, numbers or readable signage; logos, watermarks, borders, frames, UI; close-up faces or posed characters (people are small background figures for scale only); crowds standing in rows or grids, identical or uniformly cloaked figures, figures all facing the camera, army-like formations (unless the place is a military parade or drill); modern objects (cars, power lines, plastic, neon, guns, steam locomotives); churches, crosses, altars, shrines or religious symbols; architecture that reads as fully Indian, Chinese, Japanese or Persian; cartoon or painterly look; oversaturated colour.

Subject: The Old Beasts' Meadow — where retired war-mounts live out their days.
What it is: A sheltered green valley for retired war-mounts; veterans come and sit with the animals that carried them.
Composition: A peaceful green valley meadow with old war-horses, a lame armoured rhino without armour and a white-muzzled dire wolf grazing and dozing, a few old soldiers among them.
Camera angle: at ground level from the meadow gate, the scene stretching ahead. (Similar places use different angles — keep to this one.)

Little moments of life: a foal-sized pony bossing the old warhorses around.
Environment: gentle green uplands between the capital and the forest — rolling meadows, stone walls, a stream, wide sky.
Architecture: Starhaven's established look, matching the other images of the kingdom — a dark, grand high-fantasy stone city: weathered grey and charcoal stone, soaring towers and slender spires, pointed and round arches, grand domed halls, clock towers, steep dark slate roofs, wrought-iron lanterns, heavy carved detail, mist between the buildings and mountains on the horizon. Subtle accents from the kingdom's many peoples appear only in details, never taking over: some carved stone balconies and scalloped arches, a few blue-tiled domes, the odd gently upturned roof edge on a market hall, dwarven geometric reliefs and bronze fittings, elven curved woodwork and climbing ivy. People: a natural, varied, sparse-to-moderate crowd seen small — mixed clothes and colours, different ages and a real, clearly visible mix of races — at least half of them obviously not human: pointed-eared elves, short stocky bearded dwarves, green- and grey-skinned tusked orcs, big hulking grey-hided trolls, horned and furred beastmen, little halflings — side by side with humans, each race easy to tell apart at a glance — this is a fantasy adventurers' world, so a few adventurers in worn armour with swords, axes, bows and staves mingle with townsfolk; walking, talking, carrying things and browsing in every direction, in loose groups and gaps. Never in rows, never uniform cloaks, never all facing the camera. Clothing true to the medieval era — wool, linen, leather and fur; tunics, hoods, cloaks, aprons and boots — never modern dress.
Who lives here (Compendium → Peoples → Who lives where, “Beast-breeding grounds”): mostly Humans, wood elves and Wildblood dwarves; also Beastkin handlers, halflings, orcs. Handlers, breeders and riders working close to the animals, in sturdy work clothes. Still a mixed kingdom — several races clearly visible.
Crowd note: avoid rows of identical hooded robes or cloaks standing still and facing the same way. People wear varied everyday clothes in real colours, are of clearly different races, and are busy — walking, talking, carrying, working, laughing — in loose groups facing every direction.

Lighting and time of day: overcast midday, soft even light, rich colour in the stone and wood.
Mood: abundant, peaceful, rooted in the land.
Magic: magic is real and everyday in this world — show it subtly where it fits: rune-lit doors and seals, softly glowing mana lamps, enchanted objects, a mage at work with faint light at their hands. Never portals or rift gates.
People: a natural, varied, sparse-to-moderate crowd seen small — mixed clothes and colours, different ages and a real, clearly visible mix of races — at least half of them obviously not human: pointed-eared elves, short stocky bearded dwarves, green- and grey-skinned tusked orcs, big hulking grey-hided trolls, horned and furred beastmen, little halflings — side by side with humans, each race easy to tell apart at a glance — this is a fantasy adventurers' world, so a few adventurers in worn armour with swords, axes, bows and staves mingle with townsfolk; walking, talking, carrying things and browsing in every direction, in loose groups and gaps. Never in rows, never uniform cloaks, never all facing the camera. Clothing true to the medieval era — wool, linen, leather and fur; tunics, hoods, cloaks, aprons and boots — never modern dress.
Who lives here (Compendium → Peoples → Who lives where, “Beast-breeding grounds”): mostly Humans, wood elves and Wildblood dwarves; also Beastkin handlers, halflings, orcs. Handlers, breeders and riders working close to the animals, in sturdy work clothes. Still a mixed kingdom — several races clearly visible.

## MOONFALL LAKE  (1)  · historic
########################################################################

=== The Mirror of Yesterday ===
Save as: starhaven_places_image/themirrorofyesterday.png
Size: 1536 x 1024 px (landscape 3:2)
Group: Moonfall Lake   Kind: Enchanted pool
PROMPT:
Image size: 1536 x 1024 pixels, landscape orientation, 3:2 aspect ratio.
Style: photorealistic dark-fantasy environment art, like a cinematic film still from a high-budget fantasy epic. Desaturated, moody colour grade with warm practical light wherever the place is lived-in (lanterns, hearths, lit windows). Extremely detailed textures on stone, timber, iron, glass, cloth and foliage; atmospheric depth with haze between foreground, midground and background; natural lens, no fisheye, no tilt-shift.
Subject: The Mirror of Yesterday — enchanted pool, in Moonfall Lake.
What it is: A small still pool near Moonfall Lake that reflects not the sky above it, but the sky as it was exactly one day ago.
More about it: People come to see yesterday's weather, yesterday's birds, and — if they are very still — yesterday's people standing where they stand now. Widows and widowers come here on the day after a funeral. The Veil Watch has studied it twice and found nothing wrong with it. They left it alone.
Wider setting — Moonfall Lake: A lake on the Greywood boundary, site of the Year 1042 purification — a ring of silver light that burst across the water like a shockwave of living moonlight and climbed the corrupted trees for hundreds of meters.
Composition: A view of a small, perfectly still forest pool whose reflection shows a different sky from the real one.
Details to include: a small perfectly still forest pool whose reflection shows a stormy sky and rain while the real sky above is clear; a widow kneeling at the edge looking at a faint figure reflected beside her that isn't there; ferns and moss around the pool. Little moments of life: a leaf falling on the real water that doesn't appear in the reflection.
Environment: ancient forest — towering moss-covered trees, ferns and roots, timber and living-wood buildings grown into the trees, green-gold light through the canopy, drifting motes.
Architecture: Greenveil signature — a wealthy, thriving forest city of 45,000, grown rather than built: great halls, homes and towers shaped from living ancient trees, with fine carved dark-timber galleries, balconies and spiral stairs wound around huge trunks, high bridges and walkways between the trees, leaded and coloured-glass windows glowing warm, steep shingled and moss-green roofs, polished wood, bronze lanterns and fittings, flower boxes and tended gardens, well-kept stone-flagged paths among the roots. Prosperous, elegant and well cared for — never poor, ramshackle, primitive or a tribal village. Elven curved woodwork and dwarven bronze touches in the details; it stays one consistent dark-fantasy kingdom.
Lighting and time of day: clear calm morning, with the stormy reflection.
Mood: quiet, tender, haunting.

Wider setting — Skyperch: Built specifically to meet Zephyros's architectural requirements — the landing platforms are sized for an elder dragon several hundred years old, 40+ wingspan. Aurelia designed the specs.
Composition: The highest platform on the mountain, a great storm dragon resting on it alone with his rider.
Camera angle: from a lower ledge, looking up at the platform. (Similar places use different angles — keep to this one.)
Details to include: a high platform at the very summit, an elder storm dragon with blue-grey scales and lightning crackling faintly along his spine curled on it, a slim young storm elf with a spear sitting against his side, clouds below them. Little moments of life: the dragon's tail wrapped around her protectively.
Environment: high mountain country — rugged peaks and cliffs, wind-bent grass, grey stone buildings with slate roofs, low clouds drifting through the valleys, crisp thin light.
Architecture: rural Starhaven, true to a real medieval countryside — modest timber-framed and stone cottages, barns and workshops, thatched or slate roofs, a stone well, a watermill or windmill where fitting, dry-stone walls, wooden fences and muddy cart tracks; buildings of one or two storeys, made from the local stone and timber the environment above describes. Weathered, lived-in and practical, never grand: no towers, spires, domes or palace-like halls. Lighting and time of day: evening, with warm amber light spilling from the windows onto snow.
Mood: powerful, alive, a working bond between rider and beast.
Magic: magic is real and everyday in this world — show it subtly where it fits: rune-lit doors and seals, softly glowing mana lamps, enchanted objects, a mage at work with faint light at their hands. Never portals or rift gates.
People: a natural, varied, sparse-to-moderate crowd seen small — mixed clothes and colours, different ages and a real, clearly visible mix of races — at least half of them obviously not human: pointed-eared elves, short stocky bearded dwarves, green- and grey-skinned tusked orcs, big hulking grey-hided trolls, horned and furred beastmen, little halflings — side by side with humans, each race easy to tell apart at a glance — this is a fantasy adventurers' world, so a few adventurers in worn armour with swords, axes, bows and staves mingle with townsfolk; walking, talking, carrying things and browsing in every direction, in loose groups and gaps. Never in rows, never uniform cloaks, never all facing the camera. Clothing true to the medieval era — wool, linen, leather and fur; tunics, hoods, cloaks, aprons and boots — never modern dress.

Crowd note: avoid rows of identical hooded robes or cloaks standing still and facing the same way. People wear varied everyday clothes in real colours, are of clearly different races, and are busy — walking, talking, carrying, working, laughing — in loose groups facing every direction.
Creature and design notes: Zephyros is a long serpentine Asiatic storm dragon, centuries old, with lightning in the clouds around him — the only true dragon in the kingdom.

Subject: The Sky Anchor — chained floating island, in Skyperch.
What it is: A small island of rock and grass floating above Skyperch, held in place by four enormous iron chains anchored into the cliffs — a griffin pasture in the sky.
More about it: Nobody knows why it floats; it was already there when Skyperch was built. The chains are inspected every day by riders who walk along them. Young griffins are raised on the island because they learn to fly faster when the ground is already in the air. On windy days the whole island creaks against its chains like a ship at mooring.
Wider setting — Skyperch: Built specifically to meet Zephyros's architectural requirements — the landing platforms are sized for an elder dragon several hundred years old, 40+ wingspan. Aurelia designed the specs. Zephyros indicated his preferences by standing in specific places until someone wrote them down.

Subject: Lake Starmere — river (Near Starhold — the lake district east of the capital).
What it is: A broad blue lake an hour from the capital, ringed with summer houses, boat-hire jetties and lakeside hotels. Starhold's summer playground.
More about it: In summer half the capital rows, swims or picnics on Lake Starmere. The Lakeside Hotel's summer gala is the hardest invitation in the kingdom to get.
Composition: A wide waterside view with the water filling the lower third of the frame, the place along the bank in the midground and hills or rooftops behind.
Camera angle: from a jetty on the lake, low over the water. (Similar places use different angles — keep to this one.)

Environment: the heart of the kingdom — green river country, broad rivers and tree-lined avenues, iron lanterns, well-kept roads, soft temperate light.
Architecture: Starhaven's established look, matching the other images of the kingdom — a dark, grand high-fantasy stone city: weathered grey and charcoal stone, soaring towers and slender spires, pointed and round arches, grand domed halls, clock towers, steep dark slate roofs, wrought-iron lanterns, heavy carved detail, mist between the buildings and mountains on the horizon. Subtle accents from the kingdom's many peoples appear only in details, never taking over: some carved stone balconies and scalloped arches, a few blue-tiled domes, the odd gently upturned roof edge on a market hall, dwarven geometric reliefs and bronze fittings, elven curved woodwork and climbing ivy. === The Mirror Lakes ===
Save as: starhaven_places_image/themirrorlakes.png
Size: 1536 x 1024 px (landscape 3:2)
Group: Atlas — The Mirror Lakes   Kind: river
PROMPT:
Image size: 1536 x 1024 pixels, landscape orientation, 3:2 aspect ratio.

Subject: The Mirror Lakes — river (Greypeak Highlands — above Stonemarsh).
What it is: A chain of still mountain lakes high in the Greypeak Highlands that reflect the peaks perfectly on calm mornings.
More about it: Tessa Skarne launched her 14,000-foot griffin altitude attempt from the Mirror Lakes; Bram Ashford was the only witness. Climbers camp here on the way up.
Composition: A wide waterside view with the water filling the lower third of the frame, the place along the bank in the midground and hills or rooftops behind.
Camera angle: from across water or an open square, with its reflection or a wide foreground. (Similar places use different angles — keep to this one.)
Details to include: a chain of perfectly still high mountain lakes reflecting snowy peaks like mirrors, a few climbers' tents by the lowest lake, a griffin flying high above. Little moments of life: a climber washing his face in the icy water.
Environment: high mountain country — rugged peaks and cliffs, wind-bent grass, grey stone buildings with slate roofs, low clouds drifting through the valleys, crisp thin light.
Architecture: mostly the natural landscape; any buildings that appear are few and small, rural Starhaven, true to a real medieval countryside — modest timber-framed and stone cottages, barns and workshops, thatched or slate roofs, a stone well, a watermill or windmill where fitting, dry-stone walls, wooden fences and muddy cart tracks; buildings of one or two storeys, made from the local stone and timber the environment above describes. Weathered, lived-in and practical, never grand: no towers, spires, domes or palace-like halls. Small touches from the kingdom's peoples are fine (dwarven stonework, elven carved wood) but it stays one consistent dark-fantasy kingdom.
Lighting and time of day: calm clear early morning, perfect reflections.
Mood: calm, breezy, open.
Magic: magic is real and everyday in this world — show it subtly where it fits: rune-lit doors and seals, softly glowing mana lamps, enchanted objects, a mage at work with faint light at their hands. Never portals or rift gates.

=== The Thornwater ===
Save as: starhaven_places_image/thethornwater.png
Size: 1536 x 1024 px (landscape 3:2)
Group: Atlas — The Thornwater   Kind: river
PROMPT:
Image size: 1536 x 1024 pixels, landscape orientation, 3:2 aspect ratio.
Style: photorealistic dark-fantasy environment art, like a cinematic film still from a high-budget fantasy epic. Desaturated, moody colour grade with warm practical light wherever the place is lived-in (lanterns, hearths, lit windows). Extremely detailed textures on stone, timber, iron, glass, cloth and foliage; atmospheric depth with haze between foreground, midground and background; natural lens, no fisheye, no tilt-shift.
Subject: The Thornwater — river (Greenveil forest → Mendlewood → Flaxford → Dyers' Reach → the Ashen above Starhold).
What it is: A clear forest river that rises in Greenveil, runs past Mendlewood's herb gardens and Flaxford's blue fields, turns blue, red and yellow below Dyers' Reach, and joins the Ashen above Starhold.
More about it: Millwater's fast stream feeds it. Children in Starhold are told the Ashen is striped on dye days because the Thornwater is in a good mood.
Composition: A wide waterside view with the water filling the lower third of the frame, the place along the bank in the midground and hills or rooftops behind.
Camera angle: from a stone bridge over the river. (Similar places use different angles — keep to this one.)
Details to include: a clear forest river winding from the trees past herb gardens and blue flax fields, then running in bands of blue, red and yellow below a dyers' village, barges on it, children pointing at the colours. Little moments of life: a heron standing in the blue stripe.
Environment: ancient forest — towering moss-covered trees, ferns and roots, timber and living-wood buildings grown into the trees, green-gold light through the canopy, drifting motes.
Architecture: Starhaven's established look, matching the other images of the kingdom — a dark, grand high-fantasy stone city: weathered grey and charcoal stone, soaring towers and slender spires, pointed and round arches, grand domed halls, clock towers, steep dark slate roofs, wrought-iron lanterns, heavy carved detail, mist between the buildings and mountains on the horizon. Subtle accents from the kingdom's many peoples appear only in details, never taking over: some carved stone balconies and scalloped arches, a few blue-tiled domes, the odd gently upturned roof edge on a market hall, dwarven geometric reliefs and bronze fittings, elven curved woodwork and climbing ivy. It must NOT look Indian, Chinese, Japanese or Persian overall — it is one consistent dark fantasy kingdom. Starhold signature: the capital — a vast grey-stone city ringed by mountains, gothic towers and spires mixed with domed civic halls and clock towers, broad stone plazas and stairways; misty, lantern-lit, grand. Adapt the style to the local climate and materials described above.
Lighting and time of day: overcast midday, soft even light, rich colour in the stone and wood.
Mood: calm, breezy, open.

Never in rows, never uniform cloaks, never all facing the camera. Clothing true to the medieval era — wool, linen, leather and fur; tunics, hoods, cloaks, aprons and boots — never modern dress.
Who lives here (Compendium → Peoples → Who lives where, “Greenveil forest villages”): mostly Wood elves, Wildblood dwarves and humans; also Beastkin, halflings. Woodfolk in greens and browns with bows and hatchets; prosperous timber houses among great trees. Still a mixed kingdom — several races clearly visible.
Crowd note: avoid rows of identical hooded robes or cloaks standing still and facing the same way. People wear varied everyday clothes in real colours, are of clearly different races, and are busy — walking, talking, carrying, working, laughing — in loose groups facing every direction.

More about it: Couples cross the Lovers' Bridge together and tie a ribbon to the old cherry at its far end. In blossom week the petals cover the lake pink, and the garden is the most romantic place in the kingdom.
Composition: Seen from the riverbank or gorge side at a low three-quarter angle, so the bridge itself is the subject: its arches, piers and full span crossing the frame, with the water (and its reflection) or the gorge filling the lower part of the image and the far bank behind. Little moments of life among the crowd: a bargeman waving to children on the bank; an angler pulling in a fish while friends cheer.
Environment: the heart of the kingdom — green river country, broad rivers and tree-lined avenues, iron lanterns, well-kept roads, soft temperate light.

What it is: The railway lines linking the cities and the larger towns, with the dawn produce trains, the freight lines and the Iron Road Corps' military trains.
More about it: The egg, milk, fish and fruit trains reach Starhold before dawn; the freight trains run all night.
Composition: A long-lens view following the road, rail line or route as it winds across the landscape into the distance, travellers and traffic along it.

Architecture: Starhaven's established look, matching the other images of the kingdom — a dark, grand high-fantasy stone city: weathered grey and charcoal stone, soaring towers and slender spires, pointed and round arches, grand domed halls, clock towers, steep dark slate roofs, wrought-iron lanterns, heavy carved detail, mist between the buildings and mountains on the horizon. Crowd note: avoid rows of identical hooded robes or cloaks standing still and facing the same way. People wear varied everyday clothes in real colours, are of clearly different races, and are busy — walking, talking, carrying, working, laughing — in loose groups facing every direction.
Creature and design notes: This is the ARCANE RAILWAY, powered by mana, not steam: no steam locomotives, no smokestacks, no chimneys, no coal, no smoke or steam clouds. The trains are sleek, streamlined carriages of dark iron, brass and polished wood, drawn by an engine car with a large faceted resonance crystal set in a brass housing at its front, glowing soft blue-white; faint rune-lines along their sides. The rails are dark iron inlaid with thin channels of softly glowing blue runes, with tall brass-and-stone crystal pylons at intervals that hum with stored mana. The magic is restrained and elegant — a soft, steady glow and a faint shimmer under the carriages — not neon ribbons, swirls or lightning. Trains run on the rails, never floating free.
Avoid: a cast of only humans; people posing together like a group portrait; portals, rift gates or glowing gateways; any text, letters, numbers or readable signage; logos, watermarks, borders, frames, UI; close-up faces or posed characters (people are small background figures for scale only); crowds standing in rows or grids, identical or uniformly cloaked figures, figures all facing the camera, army-like formations (unless the place is a military parade or drill); modern objects (cars, power lines, plastic, neon, guns, steam locomotives); churches, crosses, altars, shrines or religious symbols; architecture that reads as fully Indian, Chinese, Japanese or Persian; cartoon or painterly look; oversaturated colour.

Subject: The Freight Yards — rail yards, in the trade route The Iron Rail Lines (Kingdom-wide — Starhold Central Station hub).
What it is: Marshalling yards outside Starhold Central Station.
Wider setting — The Iron Rail Lines: The railway lines linking the cities and the larger towns, with the dawn produce trains, the freight lines and the Iron Road Corps' military trains.
Composition: A long-lens view following the road, rail line or route as it winds across the landscape into the distance, travellers and traffic along it.
Camera angle: from the bank of a river or pond, the scene across the water. (Similar places use different angles — keep to this one.)
Details to include: wagons and carts on the road, barges where there is water, and on the rail lines the kingdom's arcane railway — carriages drawn by a crystal-powered engine glowing faintly blue (no smoke, no steam, no locomotive chimney); waystations, mileposts, dust on the road, the landscape changing along its length. Little moments of life among the crowd: a merchant and a customer haggling with big gestures; an apprentice dropping a stack of parcels.
Environment: the heart of the kingdom — green river country, broad rivers and tree-lined avenues, iron lanterns, well-kept roads, soft temperate light.
Architecture: Starhaven's established look, matching the other images of the kingdom — a dark, grand high-fantasy stone city: weathered grey and charcoal stone, soaring towers and slender spires, pointed and round arches, grand domed halls, clock towers, steep dark slate roofs, wrought-iron lanterns, heavy carved detail, mist between the buildings and mountains on the horizon. Subtle accents from the kingdom's many peoples appear only in details, never taking over: some carved stone balconies and scalloped arches, a few blue-tiled domes, the odd gently upturned roof edge on a market hall, dwarven geometric reliefs and bronze fittings, elven curved woodwork and climbing ivy. It must NOT look Indian, Chinese, Japanese or Persian overall — it is one consistent dark fantasy kingdom. Starhold signature: the capital — a vast grey-stone city ringed by mountains, gothic towers and spires mixed with domed civic halls and clock towers, broad stone plazas and stairways; misty, lantern-lit, grand.
Lighting and time of day: a light drizzle, damp air, people with hoods up.
Mood: journey, trade, connection.
Magic: magic is real and everyday in this world — show it subtly where it fits: rune-lit doors and seals, softly glowing mana lamps, enchanted objects, a mage at work with faint light at their hands. Never portals or rift gates.
People: a natural, varied, sparse-to-moderate crowd seen small — mixed clothes and colours, different ages and a real, clearly visible mix of races — at least half of them obviously not human: pointed-eared elves, short stocky bearded dwarves, green- and grey-skinned tusked orcs, big hulking grey-hided trolls, horned and furred beastmen, little halflings — side by side with humans, each race easy to tell apart at a glance — this is a fantasy adventurers' world, so a few adventurers in worn armour with swords, axes, bows and staves mingle with townsfolk; walking, talking, carrying things and browsing in every direction, in loose groups and gaps. Never in rows, never uniform cloaks, never all facing the camera. Clothing true to the medieval era — wool, linen, leather and fur; tunics, hoods, cloaks, aprons and boots — never modern dress.
Who lives here (Compendium → Peoples → Who lives where, “Roads, rivers & travel”): mostly Every race; also Traders, carters, bargefolk, soldiers, pilgrims, adventurers. A moving, mixed crowd of travellers; Karath caravans on the southern roads. Still a mixed kingdom — several races clearly visible.
Crowd note: avoid rows of identical hooded robes or cloaks standing still and facing the same way. People wear varied everyday clothes in real colours, are of clearly different races, and are busy — walking, talking, carrying, working, laughing — in loose groups facing every direction.
Creature and design notes: This is the ARCANE RAILWAY, powered by mana, not steam: no steam locomotives, no smokestacks, no chimneys, no coal, no smoke or steam clouds. The trains are sleek, streamlined carriages of dark iron, brass and polished wood, drawn by an engine car with a large faceted resonance crystal set in a brass housing at its front, glowing soft blue-white; faint rune-lines along their sides. The rails are dark iron inlaid with thin channels of softly glowing blue runes, with tall brass-and-stone crystal pylons at intervals that hum with stored mana. The magic is restrained and elegant — a soft, steady glow and a faint shimmer under the carriages — not neon ribbons, swirls or lightning. Trains run on the rails, never floating free.
Avoid: a cast of only humans; people posing together like a group portrait; portals, rift gates or glowing gateways; any text, letters, numbers or readable signage; logos, watermarks, borders, frames, UI; close-up faces or posed characters (people are small background figures for scale only); crowds standing in rows or grids, identical or uniformly cloaked figures, figures all facing the camera, army-like formations (unless the place is a military parade or drill); modern objects (cars, power lines, plastic, neon, guns, steam locomotives); churches, crosses, altars, shrines or religious symbols; architecture that reads as fully Indian, Chinese, Japanese or Persian; cartoon or painterly look; oversaturated colour.

Subject: The Rookthorn Road — route (Rookthorn → the Ashen's north bank → Saltmere).
What it is: The agricultural road along the Ashen's northern bank, wide and well kept for the heavy produce carts that carry grain and livestock from the western farming basin to Saltmere's harbour year-round.
More about it: It crosses the Ashen on the stone cargo bridge above Saltmere — the crossing every supply route from the interior uses.
Composition: A long-lens view following the road, rail line or route as it winds across the landscape into the distance, travellers and traffic along it.
